> Directory note /MUSICIANS/W/Waz/
> COMMENT: All Warren's comments are indicated by (WP).
>          "Zaw Productions" was my own C64 music group which I formed on my 19th
>          birthday, 10th May 1991. I kept it going and made various C64 tunes
>          and music demos until December 1999 when I was asked if I'd like to
>          join Padua. I was more than happy to, so I ceased Zaw Productions at
>          the same time." (WP)
>
> COMMENT: "I remember doing a little demo for Andy Startin at the time which
>          had a piano keyboard and also one of the tunes from the Merry
>          Christmas II demo (before I finished the demo). I sent it to him
>          so he could have something to play for each note when tuning his
>          guitar for his band. I managed to re-find the demo on an old work
>          disk, and thus was able to extract the tune out. I had sent him the
>          demo but only he and I probably ever had the release!" (WP)
> (#1)
>   TITLE: One Horse Open Sleigh
>  ARTIST: James Pierpont
> COMMENT: Same as /MUSICIANS/W/Waz/Merry_Christmas_II.sid, Tune #1.

>          wished I'd planned to originally." (WP)

> TITLE: Title Theme [from the Commodore 16/Plus4 game Trailblazer]
>  ARTIST: Shaun Southern
> COMMENT: "Another of my favourite games on the C16 and Plus/4 was Trailblazer,
>          which was actually originally released on that format (the C64 version
>          came afterwards). I liked the catchy title music on this one and so
>          for the C64 Cover Music competition at BCC Party 17 thought it a nice
>          idea to make a simple rendition, with a more turbocharged drum feel to
>          the first part followed by a more faithful rendition in the second.
>          It was good fun to do, and that's the main thing!" (WP)

>          "Zaw Productions" was my own C64 music group which I formed on my 19th
>          birthday, 10th May 1991. I kept it going and made various C64 tunes
>          and music demos until December 1999 when I was asked if I'd like to
>          join Padua. I was more than happy to, so I ceased Zaw Productions at

> TITLE: Cheetahmen Theme [from the NES game Action 52]
>  ARTIST: Mario Gonzalez
> COMMENT: "One of The Weekenders Demo Group and I are both fans of the music
>          from the game Cheetahmen, from the infamous Action 52 cartridge,
>          even though the game itself is horrible and full of bugs. I slowed
>          the tune down a little and gave it some nice SID instruments instead
>          including some arpeggios and a bit of a Hubbard-esque lead near the
>          end. Quite pleased how it turned out. The title comes from the
>          intro sequence: 'The Cheetahmen ran off... and now, the Cheetahmen.'
>          It was good fun to do." (WP)

>          suited the brief too. Unbelievably I composed the whole tune
>          in around three hours during one morning, primarily it was after
>          thinking up some ideas overnight and keeping them in my head." (WP)

>          it could do with being played in a different key with slightly
>          different instruments. It was nice to go back to it and give it
>          some fresh impetus and really make it feel quite uplifting." (WP)

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/W/Waz/
> COMMENT: All Warren's comments are indicated by (WP).
>          "Zaw Productions" was my own C64 music group which I formed on my 19th
>          birthday, 10th May 1991. I kept it going and made various C64 tunes
>          and music demos until December 1999 when I was asked if I'd like to
>          join Padua. I was more than happy to, so I ceased Zaw Productions at
>          the same time." (WP)
>
> COMMENT: "Imagine a mysterious woods where there are fairies and creatures
>          that you have accidentally run into, where the night is drawing in,
>          and suddenly you are left alone with only the breaking of branches
>          breaking the silent, blackened night. You are all alone and scared,
>          wondering what to do when you suddenly hear a tree monster coming
>          towards you! This song reflects those woods and panic you feel as
>          you try to escape." (WP)

>          City but when I heard Thalamus were planning an Arsenal FC licence I
>          changed the titles of tunes #2 and #7 to resemble Arsenal players or
>          chants and sent it off to Dave Birch. I was going to do the music for
>          that game but the project never saw the light of day!" (WP)
> (#1)
>   TITLE: The Stars and Stripes Forever
>  ARTIST: John Philip Sousa
> (#2)
>   TITLE: Land of Hope and Glory [from Pomp and Circumstance March No. 1]
>  ARTIST: Edward Elgar (music), A.C. Benson (lyrics)
> (#3)
>   TITLE: You're Going To Get Your F*****g Head Kicked In
>  ARTIST: Traditional &lt;?&gt;
> COMMENT: "A hooligan chant that surfaced rather nastily in the 80's." (WP)
> (#4)
>   TITLE: Blue Moon
>  ARTIST: Richard Rodgers (music), Lorenz Hart (lyrics), performed by The Marcels
> (#5)
>   TITLE: When the Saints Go Marching In
>  ARTIST: American Traditional
> (#6)
>   TITLE: Aztec Gold
>  ARTIST: Silsoe
> COMMENT: ITV's theme tune to their 1986 World Cup coverage, later used
>          as the theme tune to the "Saint and Greavsie" programme.
>          "The 1986 World Cup was the first one I really watched almost
>          all of the games of, and for once, ITV instead of BBC had the
>          better theme tune. Made sense for them to re-use it for good
>          old "Saint and Greavsie" later on and that was on every Saturday
>          afternoon during the football season, hence it was an easy one
>          to do a cover of." (WP)
> (#7)
>   TITLE: Peter Reid (He's Got No Hair But We Don't Care)
>  ARTIST: &lt;?&gt;
> COMMENT: "Refers to the ex-Man City manager and his lack of hair, a lot of
>          people substitute this for their almost token balding player." (WP)
> (#8)
>   TITLE: Bread of Heaven
>  ARTIST: Peter Williams
> COMMENT: This Welsh hymn is the basis for many a football chant.
> (#9)
>   TITLE: In Your Liverpool Slums
>  ARTIST: &lt;?&gt;

> COMMENT: "I was asked by a PC game programmer to do the music for the game. I
>          composed it all on my C64, transferred it to SIDPlay, then the author
>          converted the tracks to MIDI for use in the final version. Tunes #11
>          and #12 were not used in the game. Shame, cos I tried to sound a

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/W/Waz/
> COMMENT: All Warren's comments are indicated by (WP).
>          "Zaw Productions" was my own C64 music group which I formed on my 19th
>          birthday, 10th May 1991. I kept it going and made various C64 tunes

> COMMENT: "I started using JCH Editor again in earnest a couple of years back,
>          and an early sketch of this seemed to, for some reason, never leave
>          my head, and so I did the decent thing and finished it with some
>          little tweaks to make the bass fill the room on a 6581. It even
>          sounds way different on an 8580, too. Still, it featured in a Padua
>          4K entry that did well at Breakpoint. I guess it's a bit more uptempo
>          than I usually do!" (WP)

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/W/Waz/
> COMMENT: All Warren's comments are indicated by (WP).
>          "Zaw Productions" was my own C64 music group which I formed on my 19th
>          birthday, 10th May 1991. I kept it going and made various C64 tunes
>          and music demos until December 1999 when I was asked if I'd like to
>          join Padua. I was more than happy to, so I ceased Zaw Productions at
>          the same time." (WP)
>
> TITLE: Maniac Mansion, Tune #1 [from the game]
>  ARTIST: Chris Grigg &amp; David Lawrence
> COMMENT: "I was asked for the demo to compose a version of the Maniac Mansion
>          theme, with it being a classic C64 game. I first wanted to do so at
>          the PAL speed, even though it was slower, and build up a heavy bass
>          line and layer it on top. I just had to add that weird percussion
>          instrument for authenticity just to give it that edge. Later on after
>          the demo was released, I found someone had played my tune on repeat
>          for a fair amount of time on some live broadcast show." (WP)

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/W/Waz/
> COMMENT: All Warren's comments are indicated by (WP).
>          "Zaw Productions" was my own C64 music group which I formed on my 19th
>          birthday, 10th May 1991. I kept it going and made various C64 tunes

> COMMENT: "Made for the Filterless Single Channel Compo 2020. I decided that
>          I'd give myself an even tougher challenge and make it in single speed,
>          thus restricting the single channel use to arpeggios or noteplexing
>          cleverly to make the tune sound like multiple channels. It took some
>          time to sound right, but wanted a little bit of darkness." (WP)

>          and music demos until December 1999 when I was asked if I'd like to
>          join Padua. I was more than happy to, so I ceased Zaw Productions at
>          the same time." (WP)
>
> COMMENT: "All the music for this game (except the title theme which I didn't
>          do!) was done for a Pacman clone Pacrage. However, the main
>          programmer's C64 broke and so it never saw the light of day." (WP)
> (#4)
>   TITLE: It's All Up To You [from Shame On You: The Native Sessions]
>  ARTIST: The Darling Buds
> (#7)
> COMMENT: "Oddly enough, I have really fond memories of this one. I was playing
>          Creatures 2 for old times' sake and really liked the way Steve
>          Rowlands used to use this technique called "noteplexing" to make the
>          C64 sound like it had more channels. Well, I just _had_ to give it a
>          go, and at one point there does sound like five channels at once,
>          which wasn't bad. It was bloody messy to get right, though!" (WP)

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/W/Waz/
> COMMENT: All Warren's comments are indicated by (WP).
>          "Zaw Productions" was my own C64 music group which I formed on my 19th
>          birthday, 10th May 1991. I kept it going and made various C64 tunes
>          and music demos until December 1999 when I was asked if I'd like to
>          join Padua. I was more than happy to, so I ceased Zaw Productions at
>          the same time." (WP)
>
> COMMENT: "As a C16 and Plus/4 fan and owner, there's lots of great Shaun
>          Southern games for that system. One of them is Arthur Noid, which is
>          basically an Arkanoid clone (hence the name). I loved the catchy
>          title theme, and the in-game jingles soon were annoying earworm (the
>          use of SHIFT LOCK turns the music off) - but put them together as a
>          nice medley and tribute to Shaun's great game. The last part is
>          more of a nod to the original with its two channels." (WP)

> COMMENT: "Made these for a game by Frank Gasking in 1999, which ended up on a
>          Commodore Zone covermount cassette. It was nice to do something in the

> (#3)
> COMMENT: "One of my favourite in-game tunes I wrote. I thought of a sequence of
>          waltz steps, and tried to put that to music around those steps, as it
>          would be more calming for the player when playing the puzzles in the
>          game. In hindsight, maybe the other in-game piece should have been
>          written with another classic ballroom dance step to keep that relaxed
>          feel, but really enjoyed composing this one." (WP)
> (#5)
>   TITLE: Ingame [from the arcade game Bubble Bobble]
>  ARTIST: Tadashi Kimijima

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/W/Waz/
> COMMENT: All Warren's comments are indicated by (WP).
>          "Zaw Productions" was my own C64 music group which I formed on my 19th
>          birthday, 10th May 1991. I kept it going and made various C64 tunes
>          and music demos until December 1999 when I was asked if I'd like to
>          join Padua. I was more than happy to, so I ceased Zaw Productions at
>          the same time." (WP)
>
> COMMENT: "Originally, after the album music demo got rejected for Zzap! 64's
>          covermount tape, the plan was for this demo to be released in a
>          strictly limited edition of 100 copies. In the end I only distributed
>          20 of them. Eventually in 2017, 25 years after the demo was released,
>          I made it publicly available along with a PDF version of the novella I
>          wrote which I sent with the 20 disks. And yes, the spelling of the
>          word quadruped is deliberately wrong in the album title - this was to
>          derail the trainspotters in the same style that Factory Records used
>          to do for those attempting to collect all their releases." (WP)

> (#4)
>    NAME: Gun Runner
> (#5)
>    NAME: Into the Grey Matter
> (#6)
>    NAME: Hope in a Nuclear World
> COMMENT: "Yes, I admit it. After completing the tune I'd realised there were
>          small bassline similarities to Martin Galway's highscore tune in
>          Wizball, but I hadn't played the game in ages so the tune had left my
>          memory. Funny how people tend to ask me this question every so often
>          though!" (WP)

> COMMENT: "My experimental entry for the Mekka Symposium 2002 music competition.
>          I had an idea in my head to make the C64 sound like those annoying as
>          hell Nokia default ringtones, and made the tune around that.
>          Unfortunately I could only achieve this with combined waveform $61 on
>          the 6581, so the 8580 will play this wrong - in fact, you really need
>          a real C64 or HardSID to hear it as I intended it. See if you can
>          spot the ringtones I based the idea on." (WP)

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/W/Waz/
> COMMENT: All Warren's comments are indicated by (WP).
>          "Zaw Productions" was my own C64 music group which I formed on my 19th
>          birthday, 10th May 1991. I kept it going and made various C64 tunes
>          and music demos until December 1999 when I was asked if I'd like to
>          join Padua. I was more than happy to, so I ceased Zaw Productions at
>          the same time." (WP)
>
> TITLE: Sensoria [from Micro-Phonies]
>  ARTIST: Cabaret Voltaire
> COMMENT: Based more on the 12" single version, which appears as a bonus
>          track on the CD version of the album.
>          "I've always been a big fan of Cabaret Voltaire, and the 12" version
>          of Sensoria is right up there with my favourites. I'd tried to do
>          a version of it back in 2009, but didn't get beyond the first 30
>          seconds or so. It was good to go back and start from scratch, and
>          concentrate first on getting the basslines right, and from there
>          build the tune up. It also plays a little bit faster than the
>          original does to keep it faster paced too. Naturally the bass
>          sounds massively better on a 6581 for which the tune was designed,
>          on an 8580 it just doesn't work so well." (WP)
